Each cursed word requires a single word to use, using this word at any point uses the corresponding Cursed Speech word. Targeted creatures don’t need to understand your language or have the ability to hear your words to be affected.

Shout. You can shout your cursed words, making them reach more people. You can increase the action cost of a cursed word by one (free action -> bonus action -> action) to make it target all creatures in a 60 foot circle centered on yourself.

If no targets are affected by your Cursed Words, you don't spend any Cursed Energy nor do you receive any damage to your throat.

#### Quick Word


**Technique Improvement**

At 3rd level, with great training, you have developed your tongue and can now say one word in an extremely quick way. Choose one of your cursed words. The word's action cost is reduced by one (action -> bonus action -> free action), and if it can be used as a reaction you may gain one additional reaction just for using it (however you cannot use any cursed words as a reaction in said round).

You can change the chosen cursed word at the end of a long rest.


#### Cursed Energy Superiority


**Technique Improvement**

At 6th level, your superiority in cursed energy now affects the creature's abilities to defend themselves even more. If you have 10 or more cursed energy than the target, they make the saving throws against your cursed words with disadvantage.

If they already had disadvantage from any other source, they must roll three dice and pick the lowest result.


#### Iron Cords


**Technique Improvement**

At 10th level, due to the constant usage of your vocal cords, they have gotten stronger. Your vocal cords' hit points increase to 1/2 of your maximum hit points (rounded up).

In addition, whenever you reach 0 hit points on your vocal cords, you can make a DC20 Constitution saving throw. On a success, you are reduced to 1 hit point instead.

You can use this feature a number of times equal to your proficiency bonus. You regain all uses on a short or long rest.


#### Tokyo School Feature: Voice Transmission

Extension Technique (V-1)
At 14th level, you’ve discovered that your cursed speech can be transmitted through or even stored in devices. When you use cursed speech while a device is transmitting your voice in real time, such as while you are on a phone call, you may choose for the range of cursed speech to originate from that device rather than yourself. Additionally, when a use of your cursed speech is recorded by a device that could later play the recording you can choose to have that use of cursed speech become stored in that device rather than taking effect when you used it. The next time the recording with the stored cursed speech is played the effect of that cursed speech usage activates, originating from the device. If you use cursed speech while you have a usage stored the stored usage is lost, otherwise the storage lasts until the next time the recording is played and thus the stored usage is activated.


#### Cursed Tongue


**Technique Improvement**

Finally, at 20th level, you have mastered cursed speech, becoming one of the most deadly sorcerers alive. Every single cursed word you have now benefits from Quick Word.


#### Blessed Silence


**Must have the Reverse Cursed Technique cursed feat (V-1)**

Because you have mastered Reverse Cursed Technique, you have gained your technique reversal. Instead of sending commands with your speech, you cancel out commands with your silence. You can activate Blessed Silence as an action for 6 cursed energy, one creature within 120 feet of you to make a Charisma saving throw. On a failure, they are now silenced, not being able to speak and suffering the effects of blessed silence for 1 minute. On a success, they become immune to this feature for 1 minute.

At the end of their turns, the creature may repeat the saving throw to end the effect early.

Additionally, for the duration, you cannot speak and are concentrating as if you were concentrating on a spell. You may end your reversal technique at will.


</details>
<details markdown="1"><summary>Tool Manipulation</summary>
`fc03896e3efaa86cbc429a637557cfef.jpg`
A Jujutsu Sorceress flying on her chosen Tool, source [13]
Tool Manipulation allows the user to freely manipulate a tool they are attuned to, utilizing it as a focus for their extension techniques and transportation. 

#### Tool Manipulation


**Lapse Technique**

Starting at 1st level, your technique allows you to manipulate an object which you are attuned with. When you take a long rest, you may spend 4 hours imbuing cursed energy into a single object that weighs no more than 100 times your Charisma modifier (minimum of 1) pounds. After that, the object is now attuned to you. You can attune to a number of objects up to your Charisma modifier. You can remove your attunement to an object with no action required.

With no action required, if an object you're attuned to is within 10ft, you may lift it in the air and bring it to within 5ft of you, it stays within up to 5ft of you and follows you until you dismiss it (no action required), grab it or use a Free Action to control it.

As a Free Action for 1 Cursed Energy, you can telepathically control said object to lift it in the air for 1 minute. While the object is in the air, you may move it up to no more than 80 feet from you. While the object is being controlled, you may take one of the following actions:

Attack. As an action for up to your Charisma modifier (minimum of 1, maximum of 5) in cursed energy, you can make a number of ranged cursed energy attack rolls equal the amount spent, against one or more creatures within a 20/80 feet range. On a hit, they take 1d4 + your Charisma modifier bludgeoning damage. If the object is a melee weapon, you may replace the damage with the weapon's original damage. After doing all of its attacks, it may come back to within 5 feet of you and keep floating by your side.

Depending on the weight of the object, the die tier is increased as the impact is increased.


#### Tool Weight Table

| Weight | Die Tier |
| --- | --- |
| 1 pound+ | d4 |
| 200 pounds+ | d6 |
| 400 pounds+ | d8 |
| 600 pounds+ | d10 |
| 800 pounds+ | d12 |
The damage amount for this technique increases to 2d4 at 5th level, to 4d4 at 11th level, to 6d4 at 17th level, and 8d4 at 20th level.

Mount. While the object is within 5 feet of a creature, by spending half its movement, up to two creatures you choose (including yourself) may get on top of it and use it for flight. While riding the tool, the tool has a flying speed of 20 times your Charisma modifier (minimum of 1), you may spend additional cursed energy up to your Charisma modifier to increase the tool's flying speed by the amount spent times your Charisma modifier for a minute, you cannot perform this if it’s already benefiting from a speed increase, and creatures riding it take no opportunity attacks from flying by a creature. If the tool is two or more sizes smaller than the creature riding it, it must make a DC 15 Dexterity (Acrobatics) check each time it mounts it, or travels 60ft, on a failure the creature falls off the tool and is prone. If there is more than one creature mounting on an object, the flying speed decreases by 20ft for every size the creature is past the tool's size.

Additionally, at 6th level, you may spend up to twice your Charisma modifier to increase the flying speed.

Utilize. You can exert fine control over tools, such as manipulating a simple tool, opening a door or a container, storing or retrieving an item from an open container, or pouring the contents from a vial. You may control a tool in this way as a free action for 1 cursed energy.

Whenever your tool stops flying, you may spend 1 cursed energy to keep it flying for one more minute. You can only utilize one attuned tool at once, despite still being able to have multiple at a time.

Protect. As a reaction to being targeted by a projectile or melee attack roll for 3 cursed energy, you can call an object within 60ft of you to protect you from harm's way. It grants you an AC depending on its size, +2 AC at Small, which increases to +1 for each size above Small (i.e. +3 at Medium, +4 at Huge, etc.). If this causes the attack to miss, the object takes the damage. You may use this to protect other creatures.

At 11th level, your maximum number of attuned objects increases to an amount equal to twice your Charisma modifier.


#### Cursed Gust of Wind

Extension Technique (S-1)
At 3rd level, you’ve created an extension technique that can be used while you’re flying on your tool to launch powerful gusts of wind. As an action for 3 cursed energy, while flying on your tool, you create a strong line of wind that's 60 feet long and 10 feet wide. All creatures within range must make a Strength saving throw. On a failure, they take 3d10 slashing damage and are pushed x feet back (with x being your Charisma modifier times 10). On a success, they take half as much damage and are pushed half as much.

Objects and structures take twice as much damage, and the line is considered difficult terrain until a creature takes an action to spend 1 minute cleaning it.

If they collide with an object or creature on the way, they both take 1d4 bludgeoning damage.

This technique's damage increases to 5d10 at 5th level, 7d10 at 11th level,9d10 at 17th level, and 11d10 at 20th level.


#### Wind Scythe

Extension Technique (S-1)
At 6th level, you have created your most powerful extension, having enough destruction power to kill. As an action for 5 cursed energy, while flying on your tool, you throw a large gust of fast wind directed towards a point. Make a ranged cursed energy attack roll against a target within 60 feet of you. On a hit, they take 5d12 slashing damage and must succeed in a Strength saving throw or be knocked prone. You may choose to target any creatures or objects within 10 feet of the target.

This technique's damage increases to 8d12 at 11th level, 11d12 at 17th level, and 14d12 at 20th level.


#### The Watcher


**Technique Improvement**

At 10th level, you become a perfect watchdog, flying high and watching any problems below you. While moving with flying speed, you add your Charisma modifier (minimum of 1) to your passive perception. You can’t be surprised while you’re conscious. Additionally, while flying on your tool, ranged attack rolls are made with disadvantage against you and you make Dexterity saving throws with advantage as long as you can see them.

Tokyo School Feature: One Man’s Army
Extension Technique (S-1)
At 14th level, you've learned how to expand your technique to the absolute maximum. As a bonus action for 10 cursed energy, you can activate one man’s army for 10 minutes. For the duration, the number of tools you can control at once is increased to your Charisma modifier. Whenever you use an action for one of them, you may control all of them at once. Additionally, you gain a number of reactions equal to your Charisma modifier that can be used to take the Protect reaction.

When taking the attack action with your tools, you can only make one attack per tool.


#### The One Above All


**Technique Improvement**

Finally, at 20th level, you are always above all creatures, you are untouchable and may smite down your ranged fury at any point in time. While you are flying with your tool, you gain the following benefits:

Your speed is doubled.
You add your proficiency bonus to your AC.

#### Creature Manipulation


**Must have the Reverse Cursed Technique cursed feat**

You have found out about your technique reversal, allowing you to control living creatures instead of tools. As an action for 2 cursed energy, you force one creature within 120 feet of you to make a Charisma saving throw. On a failure, they are charmed by you for 1 minute.

While the target is charmed, you have a telepathic link with it as long as the two of you are on the same plane of existence. You can use this telepathic link to issue commands to the creature with the Summon Commanding Rules. While charmed, the creature cannot use spend cursed energy and cannot make any binding vows.

At the beginning of its turns or each time the target takes damage, it makes a new Charisma saving throw against the technique. If the saving throw succeeds, the technique ends.

You can only control two creatures at once. If you have chosen Tokyo school and are 14th level, you can control a number of creatures up to your Charisma modifier added twice (minimum of 2).


</details>
<details markdown="1"><summary>Construction</summary>
`f4d177b342315b8c7a16c99777808e0a.jpg`
A construction user with having created a True Sphere, source [14]
Construction, as the name implies, allows the user to conjure an object from nothing. Unlike objects created within a domain, anything constructed will remain even after the technique is completed. 

#### Cursed Energy Deficiency

Your technique is extremely powerful and versatile, however its efficiency is definitely lacking. You must spend twice as much Cursed Energy after reductions, when you use your Construction and Cursed Construction features, however it still counts as if you have spent one. This also applies to when you create Liquid Metal but not when you use any features through it.


#### Construction

Material Analysis
Lapse Technique
At level 1, you gain proficiency in all items you create with this technique. You also gain proficiency in 2 Artisan's tools and expertise in a tool you are proficient in.

You may spend one minute analyzing an object or substance and make an Artisan Tools (Intelligence) check to completely understand the object or substance, including any magical properties. 
Construction Technique
Lapse Technique
At level 1, you may convert cursed energy into matter.  Spend 2 or more cursed energy as a bonus action to create an object within 5ft in a space large enough to hold the object. The items may be created already equipped by a willing and capable creature. Should the DM determine that the item is exceptional quality or complexity they may set a DC and ask for a Tinkerer's tools or Alchemist's supplies check to construct the item. As always they may also disallow the creation of something due to the complexity or disruptive capabilities of the item. Examples of things that should be denied Weapons of Mass Destruction, Advanced Robotic's, Advanced Medicine, Space Travel, or anything considered theoretical or science fiction for the purposes of the setting should the player attempt to exceed the mechanics listed further in the technique.

Items you create have an AC equal to your Cursed Energy Save DC and HP equal to five times your level.  They are immune to poison, and psychic damage. The user may make less sturdy items to better represent the materials used. Objects you create disappear when you next take a Long Rest.

The amount of cursed energy spent sets the parameters of the object created. Cursed energy is not "spent" on these categories, more cursed energy spent allows the object to be improved in every way.

Size: Tiny and smaller objects may be made for 2 Cursed Energy. Every size category above Tiny requires 2 more Cursed Energy be spent.

Area: If the object is better represented by an area rather than size (i.e. most gases and liquids) then you produce enough to cover a 5ft square area for 2 CE. Every 2 CE spent beyond that increases the size of the square by 5ft. The object you create may cause this area to count as one of obscuring, difficult terrain, or hazardous. Creatures that move through hazardous terrain take 1d8 damage per space they move through, with the type depending on the material used.

Obstacle: Creating a small object for the purpose of providing cover grants half cover and moving through it counts as difficult terrain. Medium objects grant three quarters cover and prevent movement through its space. Every 2 CE spent beyond what is needed for the ovject's size allows you to create a wall extending an additional 5ft in any direction.

Weight The object you create can not weigh more than 100lbs per cursed energy spent.
 
Objects you create count as Semi-Cursed. They count as Grade 4 Cursed Tools at level 5, Grade 3 Cursed Tools at level 11, Grade 2 Cursed Tools at level 17, and Grade 1 Cursed Tools at level 20. They may not have any unique properties. This represents their cursed energy nature and the quality of their craftsmenship.

You may spend up to your Charisma Modifier in Cursed Energy on this technique at a time (minimum 2).

This increases to your charisma modifier added twice at level 5, your charisma modifier added thrice at level 11, youe charisma modifier added four times at level 17, and your charisma modifier added five times at level 20.

Created Ammunition: When you take the attack action with an item that requires ammunition, you may spend 2 Cursed Energy. If you do, you ignore the Loading and Slow Loading property for that action, as you convert Cursed Energy directly into ammunition for your weapon.

Weapon Coating: If you create a material that coats augments a weapon (such as coating it in white phosphorus or poison) it has one of the following effects, which only applies to the next time you attack with that weapon:
Lethal: The weapon deals +1d8 damage per 2 Cursed Energy spent.
Toxic: The target must make a constitution saving throw. If they succeed, nothing happens. On a failure, they take 1d6 damage per 2 cursed energy spent at the beginning of each of their turns. Then they make a constitution saving throw, ending this effect on a success.

Using this on a ranged weapon coats the next projectile fired from it, even if you create that projectile with Created Ammunition.
Object Attack: If you create object with the intent to have an implicit mechanical impact (i.e. creating explosives, poison, or trying to create a cage to restrain someone) then you must take the Attack action immediately after creating the object, replacing one of your attacks with a cursed energy attack. The attack deals 1d8 damage for every 2 Cursed Energy spent. The damage type is chosen by the user. For every 1 damage die you reduce the damage by, you may add one of the following effects:
Explosive/Exposure: The attack affects all creatures within 5ft of the object for every 2 Cursed Energy spent. Instead of an attack roll, everyone in the area takes damage on a failed dexterity saving throw (if Explosive) or constitution saving throw (if exposure). On a successful save, they take half damage.
Lingering: The attack causes damage over time. Affected creatures must make a Constitution Saving Throw at the beginning of theit turns, taking 2d8 damage on a failed save and ending this effect on a successful save.
Restraining: Creatures hit by this attack must make a Strength or Dexterity Saving Throw (their choice) or become restrained until the object is destroyed. The target or anyone adjacent to them may take an action to attempt an Athletics (Strength) or Sleight of Hand (Dexterity) check to end the restraint early. This may not be combined with Explosive/Exposure.
Status Effect: If the attack deals damage, then affected creatures must make a Constitution Saving Throw. If they fail, you may apply one of the following conditions: First Degree Burns, Chilled, Shocked, or Poisoned. The creature may repeat this save at the end of each of their turns, ending the effect on a success.

In all cases, the damage and effects of your attack must make sense for the material you created.
Chimerical Construction
Technique Improvement
At level 3, you have learned to combine the properties of multiple different objects. Beyond the narrative implications, if any material in your catalogue could justify an effect, then every material in your catelogue can justify that effect. When you create weapons, increase its damage category by one and choose its damage type from the following: Acid, Bludgeoning, Cold, Fire, Lightning, Piercing, Poison, or Slashing.

When you create a weapon, you increase its damage category by 1. You may also add and/or remove a total number of qualities equal to half your proficiency bonus, rounded up. Ammunition, Loading, Slow Loading, and Special may not be added. Spread may only be added to a ranged weapons with the slow loading and ammunition properties, and is Spread (1d4). Versatile may only be applied to melee weapons and increases the die size by 1 category when the weapon is held in two hands. A weapon can not be both Large and Small, nor can it be versatile and two handed. Weapons with the Special property may not have other properties added to them. 
Liquidmetal
Technique Extension
At level 6, you can create a special liquid metal for 10 Cursed Energy as a bonus action. This liquid metal lasts for 1 minute. You gain the following benefits while you have access to liquid metal:
Armor and Shield: Use  20 as your AC if it would be higher, and you gain a shield that doesn't use your hands. At levels 11, 17, and 20 you gain +1 AC. If you are wearing armor, you still gain any benefits from it other than AC.
Weapons: When you attack, you may attack with any weapon you could have hypothetically made using Construction Technique, as the liquid metal morphs into new forms. You ignore the ammunition, loading, and slow loading properties for these attacks.
Efficient Physiology: You do not suffer penalties for being Exhausted, until you hit Exhaustion 6 and die. You may also use your Charisma Modifier in place of your Strength and Dexterity modifiers for attack rolls, damage with weapons you have crafted.
Enhanced Mobility: You increase your movement speed by 10ft, and gain a Fly Speed equal to your movement speed.
Adaptive Attack: When you use a bonus action to create an object, you may use the Liquid Metal as your material instead of creating new matter. This reduces the cursed energy cost by your Charisma Modifier, but any created objects only lasts until the Liquid Metal ends.
Perfect Sphere
Technique Extension
At level 10, you have learned to create a mathmatically impossible shape: the truly perfect sphere. It has no contact point, causing it to create infinite pressure.

As a bonus action, for 10 Cursed Energy, you may create a Perfect Sphere in a space within 5ft of you. The Perfect Sphere dissolves into nothing after 1 minute or when you stop concentrating on it. When the Perfect Sphere is first created, and once each turn as a bonus action on your subsequent turns, you may have it move up to 20ft and then make a cursed energy attack against someone adjacent to it. On a hit, you deal 10d10 Force Damage.

Cursed Blacksmith
At level 14, in Tokyo, you become able to create true Cursed Tools. You gain the Cursed Blacksmith and Cursed Experimenter feats. Creating objects with your technique removes the need for tools and halves the GP cost. When you create a Technique Imbued Tool, you create an object that permanently benefits from Chimerical Construction, rather than giving access to your lapse technique.

[NOTE: Subject to change  once crafting rules exist.] 
Master Craftsman
At level 20, you have transcended the normal limitations of your techniuqe. Once on your turns you may reduce the action cost to use this technique to a free action. Weapons you created that you wield have their damage category increased by two stages. While wearing armor or using a shield you created, you gain +1 AC.

Further, when you create your Perfect Sphere you may spend an additional 10 Cursed Energy to give it the following enhancements:
It becomes Large.
Unattended nonmagical objects the Sphere passes through are instantly and completely destroyed.
It moves +10ft when you move it.
It deals 20d10 Force damage instead of 10d10.
When you would perform an attack roll through the sphere, you may instead have everyone adjacent to it make a Dexterity Saving Throw. They take the damage and are knocked prone on a failure. On a success, they take half damage and are not knocked prone.
Destruction
Must have Reversed Curse Technique
With great mastery over the Reversed Cursed Technique, you have found the reversal of your technique. As a bonus action, you may destroy an object by spending the same amount of Cursed Energy you would need to spend to create it. Creatures may protect objects they are holding or are carrying by succeeding on a Charisma Saving Throw.

When targeting a Cursed Tool, you only need to pay for the base item, not the magical effect. Successful applications on a Cursed Tool do not destroy the object completely, but instead break the object. It loses all magical properties until you next complete a long rest, and any attempt to use the object imposes disadvantage. Objects broken in this way may not be targeted by Deconstruction again until you have completed a long rest.


</details>
<details markdown="1"><summary>Blood Manipulation</summary>
`e4e17b33b4bdbe01315f0dc94dce44ee.jpg`
Two Jujutsu Sorcerers controlling their blood, source [15]
Blood Manipulation allows the user to control blood inside and outside their body down to a cellular level. This technique is treasured for its overall balance and has few known weaknesses. 
Design Note: Despite there being no lapse technique for blood manipulation, due to its versatility whenever a feature requires it anything that involves manipulating your own blood will count as a Lapse.


#### Poisonous Blood

Whenever this technique damages a creature of a different race from you by hitting an attack roll or failing a saving throw, when they possess half of their maximum hit points or less, they must make a Constitution saving throw against your Cursed Energy DC. On a failure, they are poisoned for 1 minute. On a success, they are not poisoned. Additionally, when a creature fails the saving throw for this feature, they may remake the saving throw at the beginning of their turns. A creature that benefits from the Unmatched Fortitude boon is not affected by this feature.

This poisoning only occurs if the technique would have mixed with a creature’s blood, so they would be unaffected otherwise. If you are a Cursed Womb: Death Painting, then when your blood mixes with another creature’s, they are poisoned and suffer the detriments of your respective Toxic Blood selection.


#### Blood Points

Blood Points are a currency used to power the abilities of this Innate Technique, alongside Cursed Energy. You can expend your own hit points to generate Blood Points, gaining 1 Blood Point per hit point lost. However, most users of this technique prefer to store blood in advance for their battles. You may store your own blood in any proper container, such as a vial, flask, blood bag, or similar recipient. You may use any unspent blood within 10 feet of you to fuel your techniques. Blood Points are considered spent, and therefore unusable for any features, if they are used in any feature.

The amount of Blood Points generated from stored blood depends on the volume of blood, as shown in the table below:

| Blood Volume | Blood Points Generated |
| --- | --- |
| 1 pint (0.5 L) | 6 Blood Points |
| 1 liter | 12 Blood Points |
| 2 liters | 24 Blood Points |
| 3 liters | 36 Blood Points |
You start with 2 bags, each containing 1 pint of blood (6 Blood Points per bag).

You may only use your own blood to generate Blood Points.

If you possess the Energy to Blood feat, your Blood Points will be affected by Tokyo School's cursed energy economy.

Water Dilution. Your control over blood manipulation is severely hindered when submerged in large volumes of water. While at least half of your body is submerged, you cannot use any Technique features that manipulate blood externally. The sheer volume of water disrupts the integrity of the blood, making external manipulation impossible. While submerged, you cannot use the Blood Manipulation, Convergence, or Expanded Blood Manipulation features for the duration of your submersion or while in heavy rain. If you attempt to do so, you will still spend cursed energy and blood points, but nothing will occur.


#### Blood Manipulation


**Extension Technique**

Starting at 1st level, you have begun to understand the essence of your innate technique. You gain the ability to manipulate blood at will, making you learn 2 of the following cursed techniques:

Crimson Binding. You may pull blood from the surrounding area, creating a net that envelops an opponent. As an action (S-2) for 1 Cursed Energy and 4 Blood Points, you must choose a target within 30ft of you. The target must make a Dexterity saving throw against your cursed energy DC or become restrained. The creature can spend its action after being restrained to roll a Strength saving throw, breaking itself from the effect on a success.
Slicing Exorcism. You create a wheel of blood that rotates at high speeds to use as a bladed projectile. As an action (S-1) for 3 cursed energy and 4 blood points, you form a blood wheel and make a ranged cursed energy attack roll against a creature within 30ft. On a hit, the target takes 2d8 Slashing Damage.
Alternatively, you can shape the blood into a stream, slicing through everything in its path(S-2). Each creature in a 30ft line that is 5ft wide must make a Dexterity saving throw, taking 2d8 Slashing Damage on a failure, or half as much on a success.
This technique’s damage increases to 5d8 at 5th level, 8d8 at 11th level, 11d8 at 17th level, and 14d8 at 20th level.
Blood Armor. You have learned how to harden and crystallize your blood to create armor to defend yourself. As a reaction to taking damage outside of your body, you can spend blood points up to half of your Charisma score, and half as much in cursed energy (rounded up), reducing the damage by 5 for every blood point spent.
Blood Ammunition. You have learned to control the trajectory of ammunition covered in your blood. As part of attacking with a weapon with the Ammunition property, you may turn the ammunition into bloodied ammunition by expending 2 blood points. Attacks using bloodied ammunition uses your Charisma modifier instead of your Dexterity modifier for the attack and damage rolls, doesn't impose disadvantage when attacking at long range, and ignores half and three-quarters cover.
You may also prepare projectiles in advance, being able to create an amount of bloodied ammunition equal to your Charisma modifier + your proficiency bonus during a short or long rest. Bloodied ammunition cease being bloodied ammunition when they've been used to attack with or when 24 hours pass from their creation, whichever happens first.
Blood Weight. As a bonus action for 3 Cursed Energy you can cause a creature you can see within 10 feet that has your blood on them, such as literally on them or staining their clothes, to be weighed down by your blood. The target must make a Strength saving throw. On a failure, the creature's speed is reduced by an amount of feet equal to double your Proficiency Bonus x 5 for 1 minute. On a success, their speed is reduced by half that amount. If this reduces their speed by at least half its total, they have disadvantage on Dexterity saves. You must spend 1 Cursed Energy at the start of the weighed-down creature’s turns to maintain this effect during its duration.
A creature exposed to light rain has advantage on their Strength saving throw against this effect and the amount that their movement is reduced by is halved. A half or fully-submerged creature is not affected by this feature.
Blood Spit. A dirty tactic but you’ll do whatever it takes to win, even spitting blood in your enemy’s eyes to blind them. As a bonus action (V-1) or once per round as a reaction to taking damage for 3 cursed energy and 4 Blood Points, you may make a melee cursed energy attack roll against a creature within 5ft. On a hit, their face is covered in your blood for an amount of rounds equal to your Charisma modifier. For 3 Cursed Energy as part of covering a creature’s face in blood or no action required while a creature’s face is covered in blood, you may cause the blood to burst, dealing 4d4 piercing damage to them and forcing them to make a Constitution saving throw. On a failure, they are blinded until the start of their next turn. On a success, they are not blinded. Once you have used this, the feature ends early.
Reattachment. As part of healing as a reaction to taking damage that removed a limb, such as using Reverse Cursed Technique or Regeneration, if the removed limb is not destroyed (as determined by the DM) then you may spend 3 Cursed Energy and 6 Blood Points to grab the severed limb and pull it back on, significantly reducing how much healing you actually need to do. The limb is reset to the number of hit points it had before it took the damage that removed it and you regain that many hit points.
Blood Tidal Wave. As an action (S-2) for 10 Cursed Energy and an amount of Blood Points up to your level times your Charisma modifier, you create a tidal wave of blood from your body. All creatures who enter the area around you or start their turn within it must make a Strength saving throw, taking 1d8 bludgeoning damage for every 5 Blood Points spent and falling prone on a failure, or half as much damage without falling prone on a success.
The radius is an amount of feet equal to the amount of Blood Points spent, to a maximum of 30 feet. If you spend Blood Points such that the radius would exceed 30 feet, then at the start of each of your subsequent turns, the radius expands in 30-foot increments until it matches the total radius based on the amount of Blood Points spent, at which point the tidal wave ends. The tidal wave remains centered on the point at which you first cast it until it ends.
You learn one additional technique from this feature at 5th level, and another at 11th level.


#### Flowing Red Scale


**Extension Technique**

At 3rd level, this technique increases the user's body temperature, pulse rate, and the number of red blood cells to give themselves greatly increased energy and physical capabilities. You may spend 3 Cursed Energy as a free action to gain the following benefits for 1 minute:

Your speed increases by 20 feet.
You will gain a +2 bonus to your AC.
You will have advantage on Dexterity saving throws.
You will gain an additional action on each of your turns. That action can be used only to take the Attack (one melee weapon attack only), Dash, Disengage, Hide, or Use an Object action.
When you use Maximum Output on this technique, it will become Flowing Red Scale: Stack, increasing your physical capabilities to an even higher degree than usual.

Your speed increases by 40 feet. This replaces the bonus from the normal Flowing Red Scale.
You will gain another +2 bonus to your AC.
Once per round, you can reroll a failed dexterity saving throw, and must use the new result.
You will gain an additional Reaction per round.
Flowing Red Scale Focus. While using Flowing Red Scale: Stack, you’ve learned to concentrate your Flowing Red Scale on a certain part of your body or on a specific task to amplify its effects on that part, such as in your eyes to increase dynamic visual acuity or into the circulation and heat of your blood to melt ice on you. At any time (no action required) once per round for 1 cursed energy, you may choose Perception checks or ability checks using one stat amongst Strength, Dexterity, or Constitution, foregoing a number of benefits of your choice from Flowing Red Scale and Flowing Red Scale: Stack until the end of your next turn. The first benefit forgone grants advantage on the chosen kind of checks, each benefit after granting a +2, for the same duration. You may do the same for Strength, Dexterity, or Constitution saving throws however, this only grants you +1 to the save for every 2 benefits sacrificed after the first.

#### Convergence


**Extension Technique**

At 6th level, you have learned perhaps the most powerful blood manipulation technique: the Convergence technique. As a Bonus Action, you can activate Convergence to compress and condense blood to its absolute limit for 1 minute.

While Convergence is active, whenever you use one of your cursed techniques, you may as an action or bonus action spend additional amount of cursed energy and an equal amount of Blood Points up to your Charisma modifier, choosing one of the following enhancements per point spent:

One additional damage die.
One additional turn of duration.
+5 feet to the technique’s range.
These enhancements are applied as appropriate to the technique being used. When spending additional cursed energy and blood points to enhance a technique, you must maintain the technique's somatic components.

When you're within 5 feet of a creature that uses Blood Manipulation or a Death Painting that is related to you, you may, at will, send Blood Orbs to them, allowing them to use any blood manipulation move they meet the prerequisites of. If you do, you may choose to compress the transferred orbs during the handoff by spending the same action or bonus action required to enhance them, as if you were enhancing the technique yourself.

If a creature hits you while you are activating Convergence, you must succeed on a DC 15 Intelligence (Arcana) check using your Cursed Energy Modifier instead of your Intelligence modifier, or the activation fails and Convergence ends. On a success, you activate it as normal.

At 11th level, you may spend up to your Charisma modifier added twice.

Additionally, during Convergence, you can perform any of the following techniques:

Piercing Blood. You focus blood into a highly pressurized beam that tears through targets. As an Action (S-2), you spend 8 cursed energy and 15 blood points to fire at a target within 60 feet. Make a ranged cursed energy attack roll with advantage. On a hit, the beam deals 8d12 piercing damage.
Alternatively, you can release this technique in a 5-foot wide, 120-foot long line. All creatures in the line must make a Dexterity saving throw with disadvantage. On a failure, they take full damage; on a success, half. The line form requires the same cost. You may maintain the line form, requiring concentration to do so. On subsequent turns while maintaining the line, you can fire the beam again by spending half the cursed energy and blood points, making the action to fire it a rooting action. When firing again this way the new line cannot be more than 90 degrees away from the previous line in any direction, the save against it does not have disadvantage, and any creatures in the path between the previous line and the new line must make the save. You may continue maintaining this line so long as your concentration is not broken, and your concentration is broken by performing any action that is not firing the beam again.
At 11th level, damage increases to 12d12; at 17th level to 16d12; at 20th level to 20d12.
Blood Rain. You launch a beam into the sky that falls back as hundreds of blood droplets. As an Action (S-2), you spend 4 cursed energy and 8 blood points to trigger this effect. All creatures in a 30-foot radius must make a Dexterity saving throw. On a failure, they take 6d8 piercing damage; on a success, half. You may delay the fall for a number of rounds up to your Charisma modifier, with the effect occurring at the start of your turn on that round.
Damage increases to 9d8 at 11th level, 12d8 at 17th, and 15d8 at 20th.
Blood Terrain Infusion. You infuse the ground with blood, warping it to your will. As an Action (S-2), you spend 6 cursed energy and 12 blood points. All creatures in a 30-foot radius centered either on yourself or on an area sufficiently covered in your blood as determined by your DM, must make a Dexterity saving throw. On a failure, they take 6d10 piercing damage and are knocked prone; on a success, half damage and remain standing. The pillar lasts for a minute and may be dispelled at will before that duration expires. You do not take damage from this technique if you are within its range.
You may also create a 100-foot tall cylindrical pillar in the area, elevating creatures within it to the top. Creatures that passed their save may choose to escape the area before elevation. Additionally, if this technique is used on ground affected by Piercing Blood or Blood Rain in the past minute, you may use this technique remotely from that area.
At 11th level, creatures take 9d10 piercing damage. At 17th level, creatures take 12d10 piercing damage. At 20th level, creatures take 15d10 piercing damage.

#### Blood Manipulation Expansion


**Extension Technique**

At 10th level, you’ve mastered the ability to expand and enhance your control over blood while Convergence is active, granting you access to new techniques. These techniques are only usable while Convergence is active. Any enhancements placed on the Convergence feature affect the techniques under this feature too. You gain 2 of the following techniques:

Internal Hardening. Even for a sorcerer using Blood Manipulation, controlling all blood vessels is impossible, but you have developed a method to risk thrombosis in return for increased striking power. As a bonus action you can spend cursed energy up to your Charisma modifier plus your proficiency bonus to harden the blood within a limb. For one minute, your unarmed strike die tier is increased by one for every 2 cursed energy spent when attacking with that limb, however when you use this feature and at the beginning of your turns while it is active, you must make a Constitution saving throw against your own DC. On a success, you are uninhibited. On a failure, you take a d12 necrotic damage for every cursed energy spent, which cannot be reduced in any way, and the technique ends, as you suffer a blot clot. Whenever you are forced to make the Constitution saving throw, you may choose to end the technique yourself instead.
Crimson Guillotine. You forge your own domain of carnage, shaping your blood into a shifting grid of crimson slashes. As an action (S-2), you can spend an amount of cursed energy up to your Charisma modifier added twice and twice that amount in blood points to unleash a deadly lattice of slashing force. The grid extends in a (5 × the amount spent)-foot-long, 10-foot-wide and 10-foot-tall line starting from you. All creatures in the line must make a Dexterity saving throw. On a failure, they take 1d6 slashing damage per cursed energy spent, or half as much on a success.
Additionally, when a creature targets you with a melee attack, you can create a smaller version of this technique as a reaction. Instead of a Dexterity saving throw, the creature must make a Constitution saving throw. On a failure, they take 1d6 slashing damage per cursed energy spent, or half as much on a success.
At 17th level, you can spend up to your Charisma modifier added thrice.
Blood Caltrops. You summon floating caltrops of blood that punish enemy movement. As an Action, you may spend cursed energy up to your Charisma modifier and twice the amount of Blood Points to create Caltrops within 30 feet of you for one minute. For each cursed energy and 2 blood points spent, you create two caltrops. These caltrops hover, occupying a 5 by 5 grid square, and up to 2 caltrops can be placed in any given square. A creature can only be affected by up to 4 caltrops during a round of initiative. Any enemy entering or starting their turn in the area must make a Dexterity saving throw. On a failure, they take 2d8 piercing damage per caltrop and have their speed reduced by 20 feet per caltrop hit. On a success, half damage and no speed reduction. A creature being hit by a caltrop causes that use to be expended.
At 17th level, damage per caltrop increases to 4d8; at 20th level to 6d8.
Blood Shield. You create a protective barrier of blood around yourself, absorbing incoming damage. As an action (S-2), you may spend an amount of cursed energy and twice that amount in blood points, up to your Charisma modifier added twice. For every cursed energy spent, the blood shield gains 10 hit points, which absorb damage from the triggering attack before any other effects apply. If the shield’s hit points are reduced to 0, you take any remaining damage.The Blood Shield is a large blood dome barrier around your space.
If you take this as a Full Turn Action instead, you may choose a number of creatures within your reach equal to your proficiency bonus to be protected by Blood Shield as well, and extending the barrier of blood to being around their space as well.
As a Full Turn Action when the shield’s hit points are depleted, you may spend cursed energy and blood points up to the same maximum to restore 10 hit points per cursed energy spent to the shield.
At 17th level, you may spend up to your Charisma modifier added twice + half your Charisma modifier. At 20th level, you may spend up to your Charisma modifier added thrice.
Blood Meteorite. You have learned how to harden some of your blood and throw it against an opponent. As a free action for 6 cursed energy and 8 blood points, you may create a blood meteorite within your hand that lasts for one minute. When you use this technique, creatures who can see you must make a Perception check with disadvantage against your cursed energy DC. If they succeed, they know you have created and are concealing hardened blood. On a failure, they aren’t aware of it. As a bonus action (S-1) for 1 Cursed Energy you may shoot blood meteorite at a target within 30 ft. of you. Make a ranged cursed energy attack roll, on a hit they will take your 6d12 bludgeoning damage. You make this attack roll with advantage if they're unaware of it.
This technique's damage increases to 10d12 at 17th level and 15d12 at 20th level. This technique can be used without convergence being active and is not affected by Water Dilution.
You gain another selection at 11th level and one more at 17th level.


#### Tokyo Feature: Blood Innovation


**Extension Technique**

At 14th level, you have learned to innovate with your technique to create a very crafty and unheard of technique. Choose one of the following features:

Blood Aeration. You have learned to circulate your blood externally and reintegrate it into your body, allowing for efficient use of blood points without excessive loss.
As a bonus action, you may activate this skill by expending 2 cursed energy. Rather than losing hit points, you cycle blood already outside of your body, allowing it to continue circulating instead of being permanently expended. When you activate this ability, you gain 3 blood points per hit die you possess. These blood points represent blood currently being manipulated outside your body, sustaining your technique.
These blood points remain in circulation for the duration of the technique unless the technique ends or you lose concentration.
You also gain access to a new attack using the blood outside of your body. When taking the Attack action, you may spend 1 blood point to replace an attack with a Blood Slash. You must make a melee cursed energy attack roll against a target within reach. On a hit, the target takes 4d8 slashing damage.
At 11th level, the damage increases to 8d8. At 17th level, the damage increases to 10d8.
This technique lasts for 1 minute or until you run out of blood points. However, maintaining this constant circulation is difficult, you must concentrate to keep the blood flowing. If you lose concentration, all remaining blood points dissipate. If the blood points dissipate, you lose an amount of hit points equal to the amount of blood points dissipated.
Supernova: By creating small orbs of condensed blood, you can unleash devastating explosions of piercing blood particles. As an action while Convergence is active, for 4 Cursed Energy and 4 Blood Points, you create a small orb of blood that can fit in your hand, placing it in an unoccupied space you can see within 30 feet. The orb remains for up to 1 minute. The orb explodes, forcing each creature within 10 feet to make a Dexterity saving throw. On a failure, a creature takes 11d10 piercing damage and is knocked prone. On a success, they take half as much damage and are not knocked prone. At 17th level, the damage increases to 16d10.
You may create a number of orbs up to your proficiency bonus at once, spending additional Cursed Energy and Blood Points for each orb. You can choose for the orbs to explode immediately upon creation or manually trigger their detonation as a reaction when a creature enters the explosion range.
Creatures in the area of effect of more than one orb make only one saving throw and take the damage of only one orb, but the DC of the saving throw increases by 1 for each additional orb affecting them.
Blood Edge. By rotating your blood at high speed, you can increase its strength and lethality by making a weapon out of it. You can spend up to your Charisma modifier in blood points and half as much blood points spent in cursed energy, rounded up, to create any kind of non-magical melee weapon as a bonus action (S-1), although they count as magical for the purposes of overcoming resistances and have a duration of 1 minute. For every two blood points used the weapon gains one additional damage dice (Ex. 1d6→2d6). This weapon counts as a grade 4 cursed tool for the purpose of cursed damage.
This also means you can’t create melee cursed tools this way.

#### Crimson Overflow


**Technique Improvement**

Finally, at 20th level, you have learned to condense, accelerate, and launch your blood at maximum speed, efficiency, and lethality. Every feature of this cursed technique now costs half as many Blood Points(rounded down to a minimum of 1). Additionally, every time you spend additional Cursed Energy on your features while Convergence is active, every 1 Cursed Energy you spend will count as 2 added, although this still cannot go above your maximum. This works for damage, duration, and other effects.


#### Blood Dissolution


**Must have the Reverse Cursed Technique cursed feat (S-1)**

Through the reversal of your cursed technique, your blood becomes incompatible with other blood. When your blood enters another creature's bloodstream, their immune system immediately starts attacking, making your blood form clots inside them. If a creature you can see within 15 feet of you has taken slashing or piercing damage within the last round, is under the bleeding condition or is otherwise considered to have an open wound, you can spend 5 cursed energy and 10 blood points to make a ranged cursed energy attack roll as an action.

On a hit, the creature's immune system attack causes your blood to clot inside. The creature has disadvantage on its Strength, Dexterity and Constitution ability checks and on Constitution saving throws and has its speed reduced by your Charisma score for 1 minute. A creature may make a Constitution saving throw without the disadvantage from this feature at the end of its turns to end the effect early.


</details>
<details markdown="1"><summary>Black Bird Manipulation</summary>
`f18145b8d35fe4a5d6f3a8bea8874270.jpg`
A sorcerer utilizing their Ravens, source [16]
The Black Bird Manipulation Innate Technique allows the user to imbue Ravens with cursed energy and control them. 

#### Acquiring Ravens

You can spend 1 cursed energy as an action (S-1) to force a number of Ravens up to your Charisma modifier (minimum of 1) within 60 feet of you to make a Wisdom saving throw. On a failure, they become charmed by you and obey all of your orders, even if it would harm them.

If the Raven is incapacitated in any way, it automatically fails the Wisdom saving throw.

You start with three controlled Ravens.


#### Black Bird Manipulation


**Lapse Technique**

Starting at 1st level, your technique has allowed you to control Ravens via cursed energy. You can command your Ravens by using the Summon Commanding Rules, however the amount of Ravens you can command at once is up to half your sorcerer level (rounded up) instead of the normal amount. At the end of a long rest, you can roll a d4. The result is the amount of Ravens that you find in said long rest.

Raven Sight. You can close your eyes as a free action, giving you the blinded condition unless you have blindsight, in exchange for being able to see through one of your Ravens. While seeing through your Ravens, you can use your Wisdom (Perception) skill and Passive Perception instead of the Raven's.

You have advantage in Wisdom (Perception) checks related to sight when watching through your Ravens.

Additionally, while watching through your Ravens their sight range doubles.

You can only see through one Raven at a time and may switch between them at will. You can stop seeing through them as a bonus action.

Streaming Service. Starting at 5th level you’ve developed a highly useful and highly profitable ability. You may touch a device with a screen to imbue it with your cursed energy, which lasts until you choose to end the imbuement at will (no action required). While a device is imbued this way you may choose for the screen to display what one of your Ravens sees. You may also choose for the device, if it has a speaker, for the speakers to play what the Raven hears.


#### Dark Watch


**Technique Improvement**

At 3rd level, your Ravens are always guarding you. While a Raven is within 30 feet of you, creatures cannot gain advantage on attack rolls due to not being seen by you and you gain a +5 to your passive perception.


#### Bird Strike


**Extension Technique**

At 6th level, through constant research and study, you have found out the true value of your technique. You can spend 8 cursed energy as an attack to order a Raven you're currently controlling to make a binding vow, sacrificing its own life for you to create a powerful cursed energy strike. Make a ranged cursed energy attack roll against a target within 60 feet of the bird. On a hit, they take 5d10 force damage and must make a Constitution saving throw. On a failure, they are Bleeding. After impacting the target, the Bird will die. Neither you nor the bird can use Final Sacrifice on this technique, as the raven is already paying its life to do it.

This technique's damage increases to 10d10 at 11th level, 15d10 at 17th level, and 20d10 at 20th level.

#### Raven Collector


**Technique Improvement**

At 10th level, you are a specialist at collecting Ravens, so much so they naturally come to you. Ravens now do the Wisdom saving throw to resist your technique with disadvantage.

Also, at the end of a long rest, you can now instead roll a d10 instead of a d4 to find ravens during a long rest. You may now  roll a d4 during a short rest to find ravens.


#### Tokyo School Feature: Swarm of the Night


**Extension Technique**

At 14th level, your time at Tokyo has made you realize that you have been treating the Ravens too individually when they are much stronger together. You can spend 4 cursed energy as an action to merge up to 10 controlled Ravens within 60 feet of you into a swarm of ravens.

The Raven Swarm lasts for 1 minute or until it is reduced to 0 hit points (making all Ravens die) and is controlled by you, acting right after your turn ends. After the duration has passed, all Ravens go back to being one.

You cannot see through your Raven Swarm.

The Raven Swarm uses the Improved Durability variant rule.

Swarm Power. All of the Ravens in the swarm are empowered by your cursed energy. They gain the following benefits:

They add your Charisma modifier (minimum of 2) to their Strength, Dexterity and Constitution scores.
Their flying speed is doubled.
They add their Dexterity modifier to damage rolls.
Swarm Cover. While you or an ally is within 5 feet of the Swarm of Ravens, attack rolls against said creature are made with disadvantage.


#### Darkness Strike


**Technique Improvement**

Finally, at 20th level, you have improved your bird strike technique to the absolute limit. Your Bird Strike now ignores resistances but not immunities. Also, it ignores three-quarters and half cover.


#### White Bird Manipulation


**Must have the Reverse Cursed Technique cursed feat**

With your mastery of Reverse Cursed Technique, you have learned Black Bird Manipulation's reversal. You can spend 2 cursed energy as an action (S-1) to force a number of seagulls up to your Charisma modifier (minimum of 1) within 60 feet of you to make a Wisdom saving throw. On a failure, they become charmed by you and obey all of your orders, even if it would harm them.

If the Seagull is incapacitated in any way, it automatically fails the Wisdom saving throw.

After taming a seagull, you may use it as you would normally use Ravens in your cursed technique.


</details>
<details markdown="1"><summary>Boogie Woogie</summary>
`1f9fa8c4326715d0b3f6493d350c6d3c.jpg`
A powerful Boogie Woogie user showing off his power, source [17]
This technique makes use of Cursed Energy to swap places with other people, creatures, and objects imbued with Cursed Energy. 

#### Boogie Woogie

Lapse Technique (S-2)

Starting at 1st level, you have developed a cursed technique that allows you to switch the positions of creatures and objects through the sound of a clap.
As an action, bonus action, or reaction, you can spend 1 Cursed Energy and clap your hands to switch the positions of two willing creatures, cursed objects, or creatures and objects within 30 feet of you.
A creature must have Cursed Energy or be holding a Cursed Tool to be a valid target. An object must be infused with Cursed Energy or otherwise qualify as a cursed object.
You cannot target yourself with this technique unless you are switching places with another valid target.
A creature or object can only be swapped into a space it can physically occupy. If the destination space is occupied or otherwise unsuitable, they instead appear in the nearest available space.

If this is used as a reaction to an attack against a creature and if the attack can still legally target the creature or object that now occupies the original target's space the attack continues to resolve. Alternatively, the attacking creature may choose a different valid target for the attack, but the attack is made with disadvantage.

Additionally you will gain the Object Infusion option from the Advanced Energy Usage feature early, however, you will only gain the ability to imbue objects that are Tiny, Small, or Medium size from the feature. If you ever gain the feature by normal means, you will gain its full capabilities instead.


#### Cursed Displacement


**Technique Improvement**

At 3rd level, due to constant training and practice, you have improved your technique. You may switch the positions of hostile creatures and can use this technique on 2 creatures other than yourself at once.
 
Additionally, once per round, you can clap your hands without spending Cursed Energy or activating Boogie Woogie. When you do so, choose one creature within 30 feet of you that can hear the clap. The next attack that creature makes against a creature other than you before the end of your next turn takes a penalty to the attack roll equal to your Proficiency Bonus.


#### Clapping Like Your Life Depends on It


**Technique Improvement**


At 6th level, you have improved your cursed technique once more. Your Boogie Woogie technique gains the following benefits:

The range of Boogie Woogie increases to 45 feet.

You can use Boogie Woogie one additional time per round without using an action, bonus action, or reaction.

#### Clapping With Your Soul


**Technique Improvement**

At 10th level, you have honed your skills and made a simple clapping technique into something far greater. Your Boogie Woogie Technique gains the following benefits:

The range of this technique extends to 60 feet.
Whenever you use Boogie Woogie to switch places with an ally, you and that ally have advantage on the next attack roll each of you make against a creature within 5 feet of your new position before the end of your next turn.
Whenever you use Boogie Woogie to switch the position of yourself or an ally with a hostile creature, the friendly creature involved in the swap has advantage on its next attack roll against a hostile creature within 5 feet of its new position before the end of your next turn.

#### Tokyo Feature School: Impossible to Adapt


**Technique Improvement**

At 14th Level while Kyoto merely cared about the switch itself, Tokyo taught you to time your switches so perfectly, and with such perfect targets, that your enemies are left grasping at straws, desperately trying to figure out what is happening.

Whenever a hostile creature is affected by your Boogie Woogie technique, it must make a Charisma saving throw. On a failed save, the creature is confused until the start of its next turn. On a successful save, the creature suffers no additional effect and can no longer be affected by this feature for the rest of the encounter.

A creature that has become immune to this feature can still be affected by the normal effects of Boogie Woogie.


#### Total Sync


**Technique Improvement**

At 20th level, you've learned that the greatest strength of your technique is putting your allies in perfect positions. You have become so synchronized with your allies that you can clap your hands together and move as one.

At the end of a long rest, choose one willing ally to become in sync with you. Whenever you use Boogie Woogie to switch places with your in-sync ally, they can use their reaction to make one weapon attack or unarmed strike against a creature within their reach.


#### Cursed Inaction


**Must have the Reverse Cursed Technique cursed feat (S-2)**

With great mastery over the Reversed Cursed Technique, you have found the reversal of your technique. Instead of switching your targets, you immobilize them for a short period, it's extremely simple but effective. You can immobilize yourself and 2 other creatures (Hostile or not), cursed objects or tools within 60ft. of you for 2 cursed energy. All of the chosen creatures (including you) have their speed reduced to 0 until the beginning of your next turn. Unwilling creatures may make a Strength saving throw, avoiding the effect on a success. You do not make this saving throw. You may use this technique as an action, bonus action, or reaction, however, it cannot benefit from the additional reactions given by the Boogie Woogie feature.


</details>
<details markdown="1"><summary>Sound Amplification</summary>
`Gakuganji_faces_off_with_Juzo_%28Anime%29.png`
A Sound Amplification user, source [18]
The Sound Amplification technique makes the user act as a sound amplification device, which allows them to launch waves of energy through an electric instrument. 

#### Sound Amplification

Lapse Technique (S-2)
Starting at 1st level, your technique allows you to turn yourself into a sound amplification device. You gain proficiency in the Charisma (Performance) skill or expertise if already proficient. You also gain proficiency with one instrument of your choice, and you start with that same instrument as part of your starting equipment. As an action for 2 cursed energy while holding your electrical instrument, you can make a ranged cursed energy attack roll against one creature within 30ft. of you. On a hit, they take 2d10 thunder damage and are knocked back 10ft.

This technique's damage increases to 3d10 at 5th level, 5d10 at 11th level, 8d10 at 17th level and 12d10 at 20th level.

Sound Waves. (S-2) At 5th level, you became better at using your cursed energy waves, now being able to launch multiple at once. When you use your action to launch sound waves, you can make additional ranged cursed energy attack rolls equal to the number of attacks you can make on your attack action, with each costing 2 cursed energy.

Sound Blast. At 11th level, you have learned how to focus all of your sound waves into one big blast. As an action for 6 cursed energy, you can launch a massive sound wave from your instrument and force every creature in a 15ft. cone to make a Strength saving throw. On a failure, they take 9d10 thunder damage, are pushed 20ft. back, and are deafened until the beginning of your next turn. On a success they take half as much damage, are not deafened, and are pushed only 10ft. back. This technique deals twice as much damage to objects and structures.

This technique's damage increases to 13d10 at 17th level and 17d10 at 20th level


#### Resonance


**Technique Improvement**

At 3rd level, you have learned to harness the resonance of sound waves to enhance your attacks. When you use your Sound Amplification technique, you can choose to resonate your sound waves, causing them to travel through the air in a more concentrated form. Your range for sound amplification increases to 60 feet.

Additionally, when you hit a creature with Sound Amplification features, you can choose to impose disadvantage on their next attack roll before the end of their next turn as the reverberations disrupt their focus.


#### Build Up

Extension Technique (S-2)
At 6th level, you've learned that playing just isn't enough, you've got to know when to launch your biggest hit. Whenever you take your action to use sound amplification or sound blast, you can instead start playing your instrument and not launch the attack. You can wait a number of rounds this way up to your Charisma modifier, in which then you are forced to launch your attack. You add 2 damage die per round you remain playing your instrument, and your thunder damage bypasses resistances when used this way. You count as concentrating while you're playing your instrument this way. You cannot use sound amplification's additional attacks when using it this way.

Additionally, you may use it as a reaction instead of an action if you were playing before launching the attack.


#### Epic Solo

Extension Technique (S-2)
At 10th level, you can unleash a powerful solo that amplifies the effects of your sound techniques. As an action for 6 cursed energy, you can perform an electrifying solo that lasts for 1 minute. During this time, whenever you use Sound Amplification features, you may add an additional 2d10 thunder damage to the attack's total damage. You can pay an additional amount of cursed energy equal up to your Charisma modifier to add an extra 2d10 thunder damage per energy spent.

Additionally, the performance creates a shockwave of sound. All creatures within a 15-foot radius of you must make a Strength saving throw. On a failure, they are pushed back 15 feet and are deafened until the end of their next turn. On a success, they take half damage from the solo and are not pushed back.


#### Tokyo School Feature: Musical Vibes

Extension Technique (S-2)
At 14th level, you've learned to realize that you should be more relaxed about playing music. As a bonus action for 8 cursed energy, you can start playing your instrument and feel a vibe of your choice for 1 minute. You can have one of the following vibes active at once:

Happy Vibes. Your music gives off happy and upbeat vibes, and people love it! Whenever you hit a creature with your sound amplification, they must make a Charisma saving throw or become charmed until your "vibe" ends. They can remake the saving throw at the beginning of their turns, ending the condition early on a success.

Sad Vibes. Your music gives off sad and depressed vibes, and it's bringing the whole mood down. Whenever you hit a creature with your sound amplification, they must make a Charisma saving throw or be knocked prone and have their speed reduced to 0 until your "vibe" ends. They can remake the saving throw at the beginning of your turns, with their speed going back to normal early on a success.

Angry Vibes. Your music gives off angry and rebellious vibes, and it's making people scream! Whenever you hit a creature with your sound amplification, they must make a Charisma saving throw or become Berserk until your "vibe" ends. They can remake the saving throw at the beginning of their turns, ending the condition early on a success.

While you have this active, you count as concentrating, as if you were concentrating on a spell.


#### Master of Sound


**Technique Improvement**

At 20th level, you mastery of sound allows you to exert complete control over its effects. Your sound techniques now have the following benefits:

Your attacks using Sound Amplification features ignore resistance to thunder damage and your technique now ignores immunity to the deafened condition.
Creatures hit by your sound techniques must make a Strength saving throw. On a failure, they are knocked prone and deafened until the end of their next turn. On a success, they are unaffected. The saving throw is made with disadvantage.
You gain immunity to the deafened condition and cannot be affected by sound-based effects that would normally incapacitate or harm you.

#### Sound Nullification


**Must have the Reverse Cursed Technique cursed feat**

You have mastered both the Reverse Cursed Technique and the art of music. Doing so has unlocked your cursed technique reversal. As an action for 4 cursed energy, you can reduce the sound in a 30-foot radius surrounding yourself. Any creature entirely inside of this radius is deafened. You must concentrate on this as if you were concentrating on a spell. This effect ends after 1 minute.


</details>
<details markdown="1"><summary>Rot Technique</summary>
`Eso_takes_a_hostage_%28Anime%29.png`
A Rot Technique user rotting a victim's body, source [19]
A variation of the blood manipulation technique, the Rot technique utilizes rotting blood from the user. While less versatile, the Rot Technique ends up being more destructive than blood manipulation. 

#### Blood Points

Blood Points are a currency used to power the abilities of this Innate Technique, alongside Cursed Energy. You can expend your own hit points to generate Blood Points, gaining 1 Blood Point per hit point lost. However, most users of this technique prefer to store blood in advance for their battles. You may store your own blood in any proper container, such as a vial, flask, blood bag, or similar recipient.

The amount of Blood Points generated from stored blood depends on the volume of blood, as shown in the table below:

| Blood Volume | Blood Points Generated |
| --- | --- |
| 1 pint (0.5 L) | 6 Blood Points |
| 1 liter | 12 Blood Points |
| 2 liters | 24 Blood Points |
| 3 liters | 36 Blood Points |
You start with 2 bags, each containing 1 pint of blood (6 Blood Points per bag). You can also use any of your blood within 10 feet of you to fuel your abilities, with the DM deciding the appropriate amount of Blood Points you can extract.

You may only use your own blood to generate Blood Points.

If you possess the Energy to Blood feat, your Blood Points will be affected by Tokyo School's cursed energy economy.

Water Dilution. Your control over blood manipulation is severely hindered when submerged in large volumes of water. While at least half of your body is submerged, you cannot use any Technique features that manipulate blood externally. The sheer volume of water disrupts the integrity of the blood, making external manipulation impossible. While submerged, you cannot use the Darkened Blood Manipulation, Evolved Darkened Blood Manipulation or Wing King features for the duration of your submersion or while in heavy rain. If you attempt to do so, you will still spend cursed energy and blood points, but nothing will occur.


#### Rot Technique

Whenever a feature from this Technique hits a creature, whether through an attack roll or a saving throw, the target must make a Constitution saving throw. On a failure, they take 1d8 additional necrotic damage, or half as much on a success.

The additional necrotic damage increases to 3d8 at 5th level, 6d8 at 11th level, 10d8 at 17th level, and 15d8 at 20th level.


#### Darkened Blood Manipulation


**Lapse Technique**

Your control over your corrupted, toxic blood allows you to unleash devastating attacks. You gain the following offensive techniques:

Blood Lance. You shape your toxic blood into a long, piercing lance. As an action, for 2 Cursed Energy and 3 Blood Points, you conjure a lance of blood and hurl it at a creature within 30 feet. Make a ranged cursed energy attack roll. On a hit, you deal 2d8 acid damage. The damage increases to 4d8 at 11th level, 6d8 at 17th level, and 8d8 at 20th level.

Rotting Surge. You send a wave of rotting blood surging towards your enemies. As an action, for 4 Cursed Energy and 6 Blood Points, you create a wave of blood that crashes in a 15-foot cone in front of you. Each creature in the area must make a Constitution saving throw. On a failed save, they take 3d8 acid damage and are poisoned for 1 minute. On a successful save, they take half as much damage and are not poisoned. The damage increases to 6d8 at 11th level, 9d8 at 17th level, and 12d8 at 20th level

You may make a Constitution saving throw at the beginning of your turns to end the poisoned condition

Blood Spray. (V-1) You're able to spit a vicious amount of blood towards your enemy, allowing you to catch them off guard. As an action, you can spend 2 Cursed Energy and 3 Blood Points to make a force creatures in a 10-foot cube originating within 5 feet of yourself must make a Constitution saving throw. On a failure, they take 3d6 damage and the blood clings to their face, partially blinding them which grants them disadvantage on Wisdom (Perception) checks relying on sight until the start of their next turn.

If they fail by 5 or more, they are blinded until the start of their next turn.

At 6th level, the damage increases to 6d6, at 11th level, the damage increases to 9d6 and at 17th level, the damage increases to 12d6.


#### Decay


**Extension Technique**

At 3rd level, you have developed an extension to kill targets from a distance. As a bonus action for 4 Cursed Energy, you can target a creature that has failed a saving throw against your Rot Technique or a feature from this technique has hit them in the last minute. You cover them in a floral pattern known to kill opponents in minutes.

They must make a Constitution saving throw at the beginning of their turns, receiving the effects of failing a Rot Technique saving throw on a failure and the effects of succeeding on it on a success.

You must spend 2 Cursed Energy at the beginning of your turns to keep it active. This technique can only be deactivated if you fall unconscious or deactivate it at will.

At 5th level, you can target a number of creatures up to your Charisma modifier with this feature. At 11th level, you inflict the poisoned condition on any creatures currently affected by the technique. At 17th level, you now pay 1 cursed energy to keep the technique active.

A creature who benefits from the Unmatched Fortitude boon is not poisoned by this feature.


#### Diverse Darkened Blood Manipulation


**Extension Technique**

At 6th level, you have learned to use your blood for offensive and defensive strategies. You gain the following techniques:

Crimson Augment. You channel your rotting blood into a melee attack, empowering your strikes. Once per turn when you make a melee weapon attack for 4 Cursed Energy and 3 Blood Points, you can augment your strikes with your blood. On a hit, the target takes normal damage plus an additional 2d8 acid damage. The additional acid damage increases to 5d8 at 11th level, 9d8 at 17th level, and 14d8 at 20th level.

Blood Shield. You create a protective barrier of blood around yourself, absorbing incoming damage. As a reaction (S-2) when you are hit by a melee attack, you may spend an amount of cursed energy and twice that amount in blood points, up to your Charisma modifier. For every cursed energy spent, the Blood Shield gains 10 hit points, which absorb damage from the triggering attack before any other effects apply. If the shield’s hit points are reduced to 0, you take any remaining damage.

If the shield absorbs all damage from the triggering attack, the attacker must make a Constitution saving throw. On a failure, they take damage equal to your Rot Technique’s damage dice and are poisoned for 1 minute. On a success, they take half damage and are not poisoned. A poisoned creature may remake the Constitution saving throw at the start of each of their turns, ending the effect on a success.

As a reaction when the shield’s hit points are depleted (on another creature’s turn), or at will on your turn (no action required), you may spend cursed energy and blood points up to the same maximum to restore 10 hit points per cursed energy spent to the shield.

At 17th level, you may spend up to your Charisma modifier + half your Charisma modifier (rounded down). At 20th level, you may spend up to your Charisma modifier added twice.

Reconstructive Blood. You can use your blood as a method of reconstructing your own limbs. As a bonus action, for an amount of Cursed Energy depending on the limb and an equal amount of blood points, you can recreate a lost limb by making a blood copy of it. It works exactly the same as the original, but every creature you touch or hit with it suffers from your Rot Technique feature. The limb lasts for 1 minute, after which it is destroyed.

| Limb | Cursed Energy Cost |
| --- | --- |
| Tiny Limbs(Ears, Fingers, Teeth) | 2 cursed energy per limb |
| Medium Limbs(Arms, Legs) | 4 cursed energy per limb |

#### Wing King


**Maximum Technique**

At 10th level, with extended usage of your technique, you have developed a maximum technique that manipulates your blood to shape large wasp-like wings. As a bonus action for 10 Cursed Energy and 10 Blood Points, you gain the following benefits for 1 minute:

Your wasp wings keep you above the ground, making you stay at least 10 feet above the ground for the duration.
You can move with your wings, granting you a +30 bonus to your speed and a climbing speed equal to your walking speed.
You gain advantage on Dexterity saving throws.
You can use Homing Blood.
You cannot use this feature and the Decay feature at the same time.

Homing Blood. As an action or replacing an attack on your Attack action, you may spend 8 Cursed Energy and 15 Blood Points to create a homing beam of blood. Make a ranged cursed energy attack roll against a creature within 120 feet of you, dealing 7d8 acid damage on a hit. If the attack misses you may choose to re-roll it, up to an amount of times per turn equal to half your Charisma modifier (rounded down). The beam’s damage increases to 10d8 at 17th level, then again to 14d8 at 20th.


#### Tokyo Feature: Life to Energy


**Extension Technique**

At 14th level, you have developed an extension for your technique that harnesses the power of decay to siphon not only the life but also the energy of your enemies. As a reaction for 10 Cursed Energy when a creature takes damage from your Decay Technique, you can make them lose a portion of their energy. The target loses an amount of Cursed Energy equal to ¼ of the damage dealt.

If the target has no remaining Cursed Energy, they instead take additional damage equal to the amount they would have lost in Cursed Energy, effectively doubling the damage dealt by the Decay Technique.

Additionally, for each creature affected by this feature, you regain Cursed Energy equal to half the amount lost, rounding down.


#### The Rotten One


**Technique Improvement**

Finally, at 20th level, you have mastered the Rot Technique, making perfect use of your blood. Your acid damage now overcomes resistance to acid damage, and creatures must make your Constitution saving throw with disadvantage. Additionally, you now overcome immunity to the poisoned condition and it now deals the burned condition on a failure of the constitution saving throws.


#### Bloody Base
